### Step-by-Step Analysis:

1. **Identify Direct Paths Between S and O:**
   - S → B → O
   - S → B → C → G → O
   - S → B → T → G → O
   - S → B → T → N → H → I → Q → J → E → R → B → O

2. **Identify Alternative Paths by Removing Key Nodes:**

   - **B:** If B is removed, paths through B are eliminated.
   - **Checking Alternative Paths Without B:**
     - S → I → Q → J → E → R → F → C → G → O
     - S → A → P → I → Q → J → E → R → F → C → G → O

   - **T:** If T is removed, paths through T are eliminated.
   - **Checking Alternative Paths Without T:**
     - S → B → C → G → O

   - **C:** If C is removed, paths through C are eliminated.
   - **Checking Alternative Paths Without C:**
     - S → B → T → G → O

3. **Determining the Minimum Cut Set:**
   - Removing either B or T individually does not completely disconnect S and O, because alternative paths still exist.
   - Removing both B and T can eliminate both paths, but let's see if we can reduce it further.
   - Removing T and either C or G eliminates all paths:
     - S → B → C → G → O is disrupted by removing C or G.
     - Similarly, removing H can also help but not as minimal.

### Conclusion:
By systematically removing nodes and checking for paths, we see that the minimum number of nodes required to disconnect S and O completely entails removing nodes T and B or nodes T and G.

Nodes to remove: 2, Cut Set: [T, B] or [T, G]
